Akien's mechanism makes that a proxy: if every device answers a health query, you ASK the running device instead of reading its class. cairn/orient's own charter names that exact failure as its founding why ('a proxy was read instead of the thing'), and the census was proposed INSIDE orient.
    · (code) ALSO KILLS CC's assumption-kill from the same cast. CC had written 'a device nobody addresses needs no receive, so the census reds on ADDRESSED-BUT-UNREACHABLE, never lacks-receive'. Akien's ruling is stronger and simpler: EVERY device, the bus and the ground loop included, is addressable and answers. There is no legitimate non-answerer, so unreachable is red universally — and the exceptions he names (bugs, things not yet built) are precisely the things that SHOULD be red. Law 9 falls out rather than being applied by hand.
    · (code) IN-HOUSE PRIOR ART, already built and already the right shape: BaseDevice.introspect() assembles intention+state+settings with the ORDERING as the contract, and BaseShim.active_page() returns that as structured DATA, its docstring reading 'the single surface the tester probes and the web UI renders — one protocol, so observability is not a separate build'.     · (code) The verb set has the same shape as declared_panes(): a universal floor every device gets free (health, status) plus verbs each device declares on top (librarian learn, cc0 send). Verbs are to the CLI what panes are to the web — one protocol, third face.
    · (code) MEASURED GAPS his examples reach: (1) bin/cairn dispatches cairn <name> to bin/cmd/<name>, so 'cairn bus health' needs either a per-device executable — a maintained list of 13 — or one fallback in the dispatcher that routes an unknown name to a device over the bus.     · (code) The established name for what Akien described ('we can also build self tests for that') is MUTATION TESTING: break one side, confirm the check reds.
